I really don't usually talk to people that way, so it bothered me enough to come see someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life recently?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. This whole thing has been going on for about a week now... ever since I saw a robbery outside the bank.\n\nDoctor: Since witnessing the robbery, have you been feeling unusually on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Just the other day, actually... a car backfired near the senior center where I go. I jumped so hard my chest actually hurt. And my hands, well, they kept shaking for a few minutes after that. So, yes, I've definitely been a lot more jumpy than usual.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have unwanted memories of it been popping into your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been. It's strange, really. Out of nowhere, I'll just... hear that man's rough voice in my head. Or I'll see the donation envelope hitting the ground. It happens even when I'm just sitting quietly at my kitchen table, having my coffee. It just pops up.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you been avoiding the bank or anything else that reminds you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. When I go out, I've actually been taking the long way around just so I don't have to pass by the bank. And... well, I haven't gone into the festival storage room either. Being in there just brings the whole thing back a little too close for comfort. So I guess I am staying away from those places, yes.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have there been moments when you felt detached from yourself or like things around you weren’t quite real?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. Everything feels real enough to me. I haven't felt detached or like I'm not myself... I'm just a bit more shaken up than usual, but I know exactly where I am and what's going on. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you had nightmares about what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any nightmares. When I do sleep, I don't seem to dream about the robbery at all. It's just the memories during the day that bother me.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I mean, I get into bed tired enough, but then... I just lie there. I catch myself just listening for noises in the house. And then I end up waking up way before dawn anyway. By the time I actually get up, it feels like I hardly slept at all.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have these symptoms been making it hard for you to go about your usual day-to-day activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I haven't been able to keep up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event the way I usually do. I'm usually right in the middle of all the planning, but I just don't have the focus for it right now. And I've stopped going to my regular card nights with the guys, too. I just... I don't feel steady enough to be around a group of people at the moment.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel intense fear?\n\nPatient: I don't think \"fear\" is the right word. I mean, I wasn't terrified for my life or anything. It was more just... startling, I guess. I didn't feel that kind of intense panic you hear about.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I blew up at one of the younger volunteers where I help out, just over a crooked tablecloth of all things. I really don't usually talk to people that way, so it bothered me enough to come see someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life recently?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. This whole thing has been going on for about a week now... ever since I saw a robbery outside the bank.\n\nDoctor: Since witnessing the robbery, have you been feeling unusually on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Just the other day, actually... a car backfired near the senior center where I go. I jumped so hard my chest actually hurt. And my hands, well, they kept shaking for a few minutes after that. So, yes, I've definitely been a lot more jumpy than usual.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have unwanted memories of it been popping into your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been. It's strange, really. Out of nowhere, I'll just... hear that man's rough voice in my head. Or I'll see the donation envelope hitting the ground. It happens even when I'm just sitting quietly at my kitchen table, having my coffee. It just pops up.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you been avoiding the bank or anything else that reminds you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. When I go out, I've actually been taking the long way around just so I don't have to pass by the bank. And... well, I haven't gone into the festival storage room either. Being in there just brings the whole thing back a little too close for comfort. So I guess I am staying away from those places, yes.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have there been moments when you felt detached from yourself or like things around you weren’t quite real?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. Everything feels real enough to me. I haven't felt detached or like I'm not myself... I'm just a bit more shaken up than usual, but I know exactly where I am and what's going on. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you had nightmares about what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any nightmares. When I do sleep, I don't seem to dream about the robbery at all. It's just the memories during the day that bother me.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I mean, I get into bed tired enough, but then... I just lie there. I catch myself just listening for noises in the house. And then I end up waking up way before dawn anyway. By the time I actually get up, it feels like I hardly slept at all.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have these symptoms been making it hard for you to go about your usual day-to-day activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I haven't been able to keep up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event the way I usually do. I'm usually right in the middle of all the planning, but I just don't have the focus for it right now. And I've stopped going to my regular card nights with the guys, too. I just... I don't feel steady enough to be around a group of people at the moment.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel intense fear?\n\nPatient: I don't think \"fear\" is the right word. I mean, I wasn't terrified for my life or anything. It was more just... startling, I guess. I didn't feel that kind of intense panic you hear about.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel helpless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I felt helpless. I mean, I knew better than to try and step in or anything like that. I just stood back and stayed out of the way until it was over. It wasn't really a feeling of being trapped or powerless, just... common sense to let it play out, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you found yourself worrying a lot about other things besides the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed anything like that. My mind is pretty much just stuck on what happened at the store. I'm not... I don't find myself fretting over everyday stuff or my health any more than usual. It's really just the robbery that's been on my mind.\n\nDoctor: During the robbery, did it seem like anyone might be killed or seriously hurt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... it didn't seem like it was going to go that way. They just wanted the money from the register and to get out of there. I didn't really get the sense that anyone was about to be killed or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. By the time I actually get up, it feels like I hardly slept at all.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have these symptoms been making it hard for you to go about your usual day-to-day activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I haven't been able to keep up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event the way I usually do. I'm usually right in the middle of all the planning, but I just don't have the focus for it right now. And I've stopped going to my regular card nights with the guys, too. I just... I don't feel steady enough to be around a group of people at the moment.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel intense fear?\n\nPatient: I don't think \"fear\" is the right word. I mean, I wasn't terrified for my life or anything. It was more just... startling, I guess. I didn't feel that kind of intense panic you hear about.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel helpless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I felt helpless. I mean, I knew better than to try and step in or anything like that. I just stood back and stayed out of the way until it was over. It wasn't really a feeling of being trapped or powerless, just... common sense to let it play out, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you found yourself worrying a lot about other things besides the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed anything like that. My mind is pretty much just stuck on what happened at the store. I'm not... I don't find myself fretting over everyday stuff or my health any more than usual. It's really just the robbery that's been on my mind.\n\nDoctor: During the robbery, did it seem like anyone might be killed or seriously hurt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... it didn't seem like it was going to go that way. They just wanted the money from the register and to get out of there. I didn't really get the sense that anyone was about to be killed or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel horror?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't call it horror. I was definitely scared, sure, but... it was more just a shock, you know? Seeing something like that happen right in front of you. But I didn't feel that kind of deep, terrifying horror. Just... startled and wanting it to be over.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Just the other day, actually... a car backfired near the senior center where I go. I jumped so hard my chest actually hurt. And my hands, well, they kept shaking for a few minutes after that. It's strange, really. Out of nowhere, I'll just... hear that man's rough voice in my head. Or I'll see the donation envelope hitting the ground. When I go out, I've actually been taking the long way around just so I don't have to pass by the bank. And... well, I haven't gone into the festival storage room either. Being in there just brings the whole thing back a little too close for comfort. It's strange, really. Out of nowhere, I'll just... hear that man's rough voice in my head. Or I'll see the donation envelope hitting the ground. It happens even when I'm just sitting quietly at my kitchen table, having my coffee. It just pops up.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you been avoiding the bank or anything else that reminds you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. When I go out, I've actually been taking the long way around just so I don't have to pass by the bank. And... well, I haven't gone into the festival storage room either. Being in there just brings the whole thing back a little too close for comfort. So I guess I am staying away from those places, yes.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have there been moments when you felt detached from yourself or like things around you weren’t quite real?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. Everything feels real enough to me. I haven't felt detached or like I'm not myself... I'm just a bit more shaken up than usual, but I know exactly where I am and what's going on. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you had nightmares about what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any nightmares. When I do sleep, I don't seem to dream about the robbery at all. It's just the memories during the day that bother me.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I mean, I get into bed tired enough, but then... I just lie there. I catch myself just listening for noises in the house. And then I end up waking up way before dawn anyway. By the time I actually get up, it feels like I hardly slept at all.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have these symptoms been making it hard for you to go about your usual day-to-day activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I haven't been able to keep up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event the way I usually do. I'm usually right in the middle of all the planning, but I just don't have the focus for it right now. And I've stopped going to my regular card nights with the guys, too. I just... I don't feel steady enough to be around a group of people at the moment.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel intense fear?\n\nPatient: I don't think \"fear\" is the right word. I mean, I wasn't terrified for my life or anything. It was more just... startling, I guess. I didn't feel that kind of intense panic you hear about.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel helpless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I felt helpless. I mean, I knew better than to try and step in or anything like that. I just stood back and stayed out of the way until it was over. It wasn't really a feeling of being trapped or powerless, just... common sense to let it play out, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you found yourself worrying a lot about other things besides the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed anything like that. My mind is pretty much just stuck on what happened at the store. I'm not... I don't find myself fretting over everyday stuff or my health any more than usual. It's really just the robbery that's been on my mind.\n\nDoctor: During the robbery, did it seem like anyone might be killed or seriously hurt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... it didn't seem like it was going to go that way. They just wanted the money from the register and to get out of there. I didn't really get the sense that anyone was about to be killed or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel horror?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't call it horror. I was definitely scared, sure, but... it was more just a shock, you know? Seeing something like that happen right in front of you. But I didn't feel that kind of deep, terrifying horror. Just... startled and wanting it to be over.\n\nDoctor: During the robbery, did you see a weapon?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't see one. I mean, they didn't pull out a gun or a knife or anything like that. They might have had something hidden in their pockets, I don't really know, but... no, I didn't actually see a weapon myself.\n\nDoctor: At the time of the robbery, did you believe that anyone there was in immediate danger of being seriously injured or killed?\n\nPatient: No, I... I really didn't. Like I mentioned a minute ago, it just didn't seem like they were out to hurt anybody. Since I didn't see a weapon or anything, I figured they just wanted the cash from the drawer and to get out fast. So, no, I didn't think any of us were in immediate danger of being killed.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms start right after you witnessed the robbery?\n\nPatient: Yes, they did. It's been going on for about a week now... ever since the robbery outside the bank.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose what brought me in is... I had a bit of an outburst recently. I blew up at one of the younger volunteers where I help out, just over a crooked tablecloth of all things. I really don't usually talk to people that way, so it bothered me enough to come see someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life recently?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. This whole thing has been going on for about a week now... ever since I saw a robbery outside the bank.\n\nDoctor: Since witnessing the robbery, have you been feeling unusually on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Just the other day, actually... a car backfired near the senior center where I go. I jumped so hard my chest actually hurt. And my hands, well, they kept shaking for a few minutes after that. So, yes, I've definitely been a lot more jumpy than usual.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have unwanted memories of it been popping into your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been. It's strange, really. Out of nowhere, I'll just... hear that man's rough voice in my head. Or I'll see the donation envelope hitting the ground. It happens even when I'm just sitting quietly at my kitchen table, having my coffee. It just pops up.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you been avoiding the bank or anything else that reminds you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. When I go out, I've actually been taking the long way around just so I don't have to pass by the bank. And... well, I haven't gone into the festival storage room either. Being in there just brings the whole thing back a little too close for comfort. So I guess I am staying away from those places, yes.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have there been moments when you felt detached from yourself or like things around you weren’t quite real?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. Everything feels real enough to me. I haven't felt detached or like I'm not myself... I'm just a bit more shaken up than usual, but I know exactly where I am and what's going on. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you had nightmares about what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any nightmares. When I do sleep, I don't seem to dream about the robbery at all. It's just the memories during the day that bother me.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I mean, I get into bed tired enough, but then... I just lie there. I catch myself just listening for noises in the house. And then I end up waking up way before dawn anyway. By the time I actually get up, it feels like I hardly slept at all.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have these symptoms been making it hard for you to go about your usual day-to-day activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I haven't been able to keep up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event the way I usually do. I'm usually right in the middle of all the planning, but I just don't have the focus for it right now. And I've stopped going to my regular card nights with the guys, too. I just... I don't feel steady enough to be around a group of people at the moment.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel intense fear?\n\nPatient: I don't think \"fear\" is the right word. I mean, I wasn't terrified for my life or anything. It was more just... startling, I guess. I didn't feel that kind of intense panic you hear about.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel helpless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I felt helpless. I mean, I knew better than to try and step in or anything like that. I just stood back and stayed out of the way until it was over. It wasn't really a feeling of being trapped or powerless, just... common sense to let it play out, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, have you found yourself worrying a lot about other things besides the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed anything like that. My mind is pretty much just stuck on what happened at the store. I'm not... I don't find myself fretting over everyday stuff or my health any more than usual. It's really just the robbery that's been on my mind.\n\nDoctor: During the robbery, did it seem like anyone might be killed or seriously hurt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... it didn't seem like it was going to go that way. They just wanted the money from the register and to get out of there. I didn't really get the sense that anyone was about to be killed or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When you witnessed the robbery, did you feel horror?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't call it horror. I was definitely scared, sure, but... it was more just a shock, you know? Seeing something like that happen right in front of you. But I didn't feel that kind of deep, terrifying horror. Just... startled and wanting it to be over.\n\nDoctor: During the robbery, did you see a weapon?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't see one. I mean, they didn't pull out a gun or a knife or anything like that. They might have had something hidden in their pockets, I don't really know, but... no, I didn't actually see a weapon myself.\n\nDoctor: At the time of the robbery, did you believe that anyone there was in immediate danger of being seriously injured or killed?\n\nPatient: No, I... I really didn't. Like I mentioned a minute ago, it just didn't seem like they were out to hurt anybody. Since I didn't see a weapon or anything, I figured they just wanted the cash from the drawer and to get out fast. So, no, I didn't think any of us were in immediate danger of being killed.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms start right after you witnessed the robbery?\n\nPatient: Yes, they did. It's been going on for about a week now... ever since the robbery outside the bank.\n\nDoctor: During the robbery, did you think you might be seriously injured?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. Like I was saying a minute ago about the others, I really didn't think they were out to hurt anybody, and that included me. I just stayed back out of the way. It all happened so fast, I just figured if I didn't do anything foolish, they'd just take the money and run. So, no, I didn't think I was going to get hurt.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
